10 Perfect Thriller Books That Hooked Me From the First 10 Pages

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

At any given moment, Scott Simone has at least three books jockeying for his attention. He’ll never turn down a campus novel with a literary bent, nor will he hesitate to add yet another mystery to his Leaning Tower of Pisa-inspired to-be-read pile — which is built upon the bedrocks of too many short story collections, how-are-these-real true crime books, and classics waiting to be cracked open again. Topics that have him ripping through pages the fastest include closed-circle murders, unhappy professors, life-questioning philosophical deep dives, so long as they feature sharp sentences, a dark sense of humor, and at least a little malice.

He’s spent so much time in book shops over the years he is, to his wife’s chagrin, continuing to build a library that doesn’t quite respect the dimensions of the apartment meant to contain it.

There's nothing like a first impression, and that's especially true for novels. The first few pages of a book need to do the impossible: ground the reader without becoming expositional vomit, hook them while keeping the focus tight, implant them into the action, and somehow establish a voice and a tone. It’s an absurd undertaking if you stop to think about it, which is why for every “it was the best of times, it was the worst of times” out there, there are hundreds of “just couldn’t get into it” openers.

But there are those that somehow pull off the impossible. Maybe it’s the way they frame an innocent happening in a way that has you questioning your senses, or embed you inside the mind of someone who’d feel at home inside a padded cell. Of course, that’s only the beginning. Because lesser thrillers go pedal to the metal from the beginning, only to run out of gas in the middle and find themselves sputtering to the finish line. The best thrillers know when to shift gears, to take their foot off the gas and ease into the twists and turns ahead, and just when to take it back into a higher gear.

The Ax kicks off with an anti-confession: Burke Devore has never killed anyone. There’s his father, who may know a thing or two from serving in World War II, but his father’s dead. He had an old Luger, which could come in handy, but Burke has never even fired a gun. Again, he’s coming up a bit short. So just a few pages in, and the reader comes face-to-face with a possible murder. But there are lingering questions, like who he wants dead and what could’ve driven this ordinary middle-aged man to this point.

It’s this mixture that makes Donald E. Westlake’s opening pages so masterful. It’s that blasé self-critique, akin to an unqualified job applicant ticking off the weaknesses in his resume, mixed with so many unanswered questions that propels the reader into the story. And the explanation for how Burke landed here — losing his job as a manager at a paper company and months of unsuccessful applications — keeps building upon that. Westlake turns murder into just another unpleasant business decision. It's a satire of corporate America that somehow manages to make us laugh at a man planning multiple murders.

S. A. Cosby starts pressing the gas from the jump in Blacktop Wasteland. Literally. He places the reader at the starting line of a race; a cocky driver revving his Oldsmobile as Beauregard Montage, known as Bug, stares him down. Bug hears something no one else does: a faulty valve, and bets on himself, to the tune of $1,000. The fact that his auto-repair shop is $800 behind on rent, and his youngest child needs glasses, be damned.

Cosby shows us just a glimpse of his cards, but it’s enough to keep us at the table. We get the two competing sides of Bug that will continue to wrestle one another throughout the succeeding pages: his unmatched ability behind the wheel and his less-than-stellar judgment outside the car. Cosby keeps raising the stakes from there, injecting such exhilaration into the pages with each getaway that it’s impossible not to root for Bug to pull it off, though the dread you feel hoping he might come to his senses grows heavier and heavier.

There’s a sinister dichotomy in the opening pages of Brighton Rock: Charles Hale is desperately trying to evade killers, zigzagging amongst the crowds of Brighton’s holiday market. Graham Greene leaves the biggest question — what Charles has done to end up in this situation — rattling around the reader’s mind. It’s dark and twisted, thrilling and contemplative. Here's a man facing almost certain death in a seaside resort full of people having the time of their lives.

Greene uses that contrast as a throughline to turn a seaside crime story into one of the more meditative thrillers ever written. It centers on Pinkie Brown, a teenage gangster and devout Catholic who believes in eternal damnation with every fiber of his being, making his willingness to kill all the more baffling. Then there's Ida Arnold, a considerably less religious woman more concerned with enjoying life and treating people decently. When the two become intertwined in the aftermath of Hale's murder, Greene rides their contradictory beliefs to explore sin and redemption.

Frederick Forsyth kicks off The Day of the Jackal with an execution. Jean-Marie Bastien-Thiry has been sentenced to death for helping plot the assassination of French President Charles de Gaulle, and Forsyth is going to make us watch. He takes us through the whole grim business with the eye of a crime reporter, right down to the traffic passing outside the prison walls. Then, with Bastien-Thiry dispatched, Forsyth turns back the clock to reconstruct the assassination attempt itself.

Forsyth almost coddles the reader to a degree in which they have no other option but to accept the extraordinary things Forsyth has planned. He establishes a factual foundation, pulling selections from the historical record while slipping his fictional assassin into the mix in a way that makes it hard to tell fact from fiction. And the intricate details don’t end there, as the ensuing manhunt is a literal paper trail of forged passports and surveillance reports.

Ilaria Tuti turns a room full of babies into a terrifying scene in Flowers Over the Inferno. Forty infants lie in numbered cots inside an Austrian orphanage, and not one of them cries. The caretakers conceal their faces behind veils, forbidden from showing the children any affection. Number 39 glares at a caretaker named Agnes, watching her movements with an attentiveness that seems unnatural for an infant. When she reaches over with a finger extended, he bites it, and the other babies begin stirring.

Tuti then takes the reader far from that orphanage, both in space and time. As that troubling scene festers in the reader's mind, we’re introduced to police superintendent Teresa Battaglia, who, decades later in the Italian Alps, is investigating a series of grisly crimes while her memory slowly deteriorates. As the two stories converge, Tuti gives us increasingly disturbing glimpses into the experiments conducted at the orphanage, and the less straightforward the distinction between victim and perpetrator becomes.

You'd think finding your mother with her throat slit would provoke some pretty extreme emotions. You-Jeong Jeong makes the opening of The Good Son uncomfortable by giving us the opposite. Yu-jin wakes to the smell of blood, discovers his mother's body downstairs, and can barely remember the previous night. There's also blood on his clothes. Yet he seems more concerned with explaining his predicament than grieving his mother. It's a hell of a way to introduce a narrator.

Jeong places the reader in the hands — and mind — of this deeply suspicious man to find out what happened. Yu-jin starts piecing together the previous night, offering explanations that seem reasonable enough until the evidence begins contradicting them. But he has a knack for justifying his behavior, and Jeong makes it easy to accept his excuses. Soon, we're questioning not only if Yu-jin killed his mother, but if he's capable of admitting it to himself. It's a bit of manipulation on the part of Jeong, who makes the reader distrust practically everything they read.

The River starts simply enough: with the smell of smoke. Jack and Wynn catch a whiff while canoeing through the Canadian wilderness. Then they catch another, and another. There are no campsites, no other travelers in sight. When they climb to an overlook, they can see a wildfire ripping its way through the forest, heading right for them. Peter Heller flips the script: he’d spent most of the opening writing the wilderness as paradise, only to turn it into a death trap in which every gust of wind matters.

It seems troubling enough, a predicament most writers would spend another hundred-plus pages wringing out the tension. But Heller pours more fuel onto the story. Jack and Wynn overhear a couple arguing and decide to investigate what’s happening. All they get for their trouble is a situation that’s far more difficult to navigate than the river ahead. The River isn’t just a thriller for thriller’s sake; instead, it explores the fragility of male friendship and the horrors of human nature, stripping away any romantic notion of the back-to-nature fantasy.

John le Carré sharpened the opening of The Spy Who Came in From the Cold into a sinister edge that cuts through the heart of the reader. An agent doesn’t just get gunned down while trying to escape East Berlin; the intelligence officer waiting for him on the other side is praying he's dead. And, even worse, he’s the one responsible for keeping the agent alive.

Alec Leamas, the intelligence officer, does have his reasons. A wounded agent can be interrogated, but a dead one takes his secrets with him. It's a horrible calculation, but you understand it, and that's how le Carré gets you. Leamas is sent on one final assignment to bring down an East German intelligence chief, and the further he gets, the harder it becomes to tell who's deceiving whom. But it's the moral math that makes this thriller novel so devastating, and the real tragedy is watching Leamas discover just how little his years of loyal service have bought him.

Most thrillers would save the revelation that a character has killed two men for a big moment. Jean-Patrick Manchette drops it into the opening of Three to Kill almost as an aside. Georges Gerfaut is speeding around Paris at 90 mph, having downed five bourbons and a couple of barbiturates. But the narrator is more interested in his Mercedes, his taste in West Coast jazz, and his political leanings than the fact that he's murdered two people in the past year.

And the explanation, when it comes, is almost as absurd. Georges stops to help an injured motorist and, for his trouble, winds up being hunted by professional killers. Manchette turns the bloodshed that follows into a joke about French middle-class complacency, describing extreme violence with the same indifference he gives to Georges's car upholstery. Georges is oddly skilled at doling out the type of brutality from which he's spent his life sheltered. It all becomes an indictment of bourgeois respectability, delivered with such deadpan humor that you occasionally forget the appalling nature of what’s happening.

Patricia Highsmith opens The Talented Mr. Ripley with Tom Ripley convinced he's being followed by the police. He's been running a few scams around New York, so he's got reason to worry. The stranger turns out to be Herbert Greenleaf, a wealthy businessman who wants Tom to travel to Italy and persuade his son Dickie to come home. Within a few pages, Highsmith has introduced a man who's terrified of being found out, then handed him an opportunity to become someone else entirely.

And that's exactly where she takes him. Tom becomes obsessed with Dickie's privileged life, and his envy gradually gives way to something far more dangerous. Highsmith lets us follow every calculation Tom makes, including the excuses he invents to justify his increasingly atrocious behavior. Before long, we're making excuses for Tom right along with him. We know what he's doing is wrong, yet we find ourselves hoping he won't get caught. And with every lie he tells, Highsmith gives us another reason to fear that he will.

Serie A probable line-ups: Napoli vs. Lazio

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Serie A leaders Lazio are forced to do without Mattia Zaccagni, but rely on Davide Frattesi and Gustav Isaksen, while Napoli hope Kevin De Bruyne, Scott McTominay and Rasmus Hojlund can end the crisis.

It kicks off at the Stadio Diego Armando Maradona in Naples on Sunday at 11.30 GMT (12.30 CET).

Few expected the two teams to be in their current position going into the season, as Gennaro Gattuso’s Lazio are alone three points clear at the top of the table, where Max Allegri is facing calls for his dismissal after a series of poor results, including the 4-1 Champions League defeat to FC Porto.

The injury crisis keeps on getting worse for Napoli, as Giovanni Di Lorenzo, Leonardo Spinazzola, Stanislav Lobotka, Benoit Badiashile, David Neres, Alisson Santos, Costantino Favasuli and Alessandro Buongiorno are ruled out.

Luca Marianucci is also suspended following his red card in last week’s 2-0 Serie A defeat to Juventus, with Mathias Olivera not at 100 per cent.

This leaves extremely limited options, so Allegri has two major choices to make over his tactical set-up.

It’s possible he could use a 3-5-2 with McTominay or De Bruyne pushed into a more advanced role in support of lone striker Hojlund, placing Matteo Politano and Antonio Vergara as the wing-backs.

Another alternative is getting Olivera on the left, with Vergara on the right, then using Noa Lang as the support for Hojlund, which would place both De Bruyne and McTominay in midfield.

Lazio have been without Albert Gudmundsson for several weeks, but the new absentee is captain Zaccagni with a thigh injury.

This does force a change for Gattuso, so Tijjani Noslin is expected to shuffle over into that wide role, leaving the centre-forward position to Andrea Pinamonti.

There are also some concerns over Nuno Tavares, who is struggling with a muscular issue, so Alfonso Pedraza is ready to step in if required at left-back.

Frattesi should take the midfield reins with Reda Belahyane and Kenneth Taylor, the only doubt is between Oliver Provstgaard and Diogo Leite in defence.

Napoli: Meret; Beukema, Rrahmani, Marin; Vergara, De Bruyne, Gilmour, McTominay, Olivera; Lang, Hojlund

Lazio: Mandas; Floriani Mussolini, Sutalo, Provstgaard, Nuno Tavares; Frattesi, Belahyane, Taylor; Isaksen, Pinamonti, Noslin

Lions place LB Jack Campbell on injured reserve

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

The Lions placed linebacker Jack Campbell on injured reserve, the team announced Saturday.

Campbell injured his neck during last week’s victory over the Vikings and remains hospitalized. Campbell released a statement earlier this week saying he has full movement in his lower body and continues to make progress with movement in his upper body.

Trevor Nowaske is expected to start for Campbell alongside Derrick Barnes and Malcolm Rodriguez.

In a corresponding move, the Lions signed cornerback Nick Whiteside from the practice squad. Whiteside has not played in 2026 but played nine games for the Lions last season.

The Lions also announced the elevations of defensive end Anthony Lucas and running back Jabari Small from the practice squad.

A value for money entry-level gaming prebuilt.

It is arguably the worst time to build a gaming PC especially with the ongoing memory crisis inflating the cost of RAM, SSDs, and graphics cards. One way to possibly work around the rising costs is to consider a prebuilt system. For instance, the iBuyPower Slate Gaming Desktop PC, a well-rounded mid-range gaming PC which is selling for a very reasonable price of $899, down from its regular price of $1,399 over at Best Buy.

The system is powered by the AMD Ryzen 5 8400F, an entry-level 6-core, 12-thread desktop processor with a relatively low 65W TDP. This makes it fairly power-efficient while still offering decent performance for 1080p gaming. For graphics, the prebuilt comes with an Nvidia GeForce RTX 5060, a solid GPU for 1080p gaming as well as an entry point to Nvidia's latest Blackwell architecture, with support for multi-frame generation and newly released DLSS 5.

This Best Buy deal makes the iBuyPower Slate considerably more affordable, cutting $500 off the price of a prebuilt gaming PC with an RTX 5060, Ryzen 5 8400F, and 1TB NVMe SSD

For memory, there's a single 16GB DDR5 stick running at 4,800MT/s. which isn't particularly fast and should ideally be configured in dual-channel, but a 16GB DDR5 memory stick on its own can cost around $300, making the system's $899 asking price a pretty solid deal. It also comes with a 1TB NVMe SSD with additional storage expansion for a secondary M.2 as well as SATA 2.5-inch and 3.5-inch drives. It is also worth noting that this iBuyPower prebuilt is cheaper yet more powerful than the Steam Machine, which currently goes for $1,049 for the base 512GB storage model. With a little bit of tinkering one could even use this system as a dedicated gaming PC for their living room.

In terms of cooling you get a single tower CPU cooler in addition to four ARGB fans, three at the front and one at the rear. The sturdy mid-tower chassis offers additional cooling fan mounting options, a glass side-panel, and a 600W PSU. iBuyPower also offers an entry-level gaming keyboard and mouse combo with the gaming PC. Remember, the discounted price of $899 is a limited-time offer, so you may want to grab it before the deal expires.

Girl With The Dragon Tattoo Remake Rounds Out Vanger Family Casting With Games Of Thrones & MCU Stars

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Growing up around performers, storytelling was always central to Emma's life. Add in a nerdy older brother who introduced her to Marvel at far too young an age, and her love for entertainment led her to pursue a Journalism degree at Indiana University, where she discovered her passion for entertainment reporting. She spent nearly one year in Los Angeles covering industry stories and fully immersing herself in the world of entertainment media.

The Girl with the Dragon Tattoo remake has added major stars to its growing cast.

The upcoming adaptation of Stieg Larsson's bestselling novel follows journalist Mikael Blomkvist and hacker Lisbeth Salander as they investigate the mysterious disappearance of a young woman connected to a wealthy Swedish family. The story was previously adapted into a 2009 Swedish movie starring Noomi Rapace and a 2011 Hollywood adaptation directed by David Fincher, starring Daniel Craig and Rooney Mara. Now, Sky will adapt The Girl with the Dragon Tattoo as an eight-episode television series set in the present day.

Per Deadline, The Girl with the Dragon Tattoo has officially added new members to the Vanger family cast, with Game of Thrones star Jonathan Pryce joining as Henrik Vanger, MCU actor James D'Arcy as Martin Vanger, and Lena Olin as Isabella Vanger. Alun Armstrong will portray Harald Vanger, Jessica Raine will play Cecilia Vanger, and Paul Ready will appear as Birger Vanger. Meanwhile, Camille Cottin has joined as Erika Berger, Blomkvist's editor.

The newly announced actors join David Oyelowo as Mikael Blomkvist and Viola Prettejohn as Lisbeth Salander, whose casting was confirmed in September 2026. Pryce is best known for playing the High Sparrow in Game of Thrones, while D'Arcy played Edwin Jarvis in Marvel's Agent Carter and Avengers: Endgame, most recently reprising the role in VisionQuest. Olin previously appeared in Alias and Nine Perfect Strangers.

The Vanger family plays a central role in Larsson's original mystery. Henrik, the head of Vanger Enterprises, recruits Blomkvist to investigate the disappearance of his niece Harriet, who vanished decades earlier. In Fincher's 2011 adaptation of The Girl with the Dragon Tattoo, Christopher Plummer portrayed Henrik, while Robin Wright played Erika Berger. The upcoming series will introduce new interpretations of these characters, and its eight-episode format may allow more time to explore the complicated relationships among the Vangers.

The series is produced by Left Bank Pictures, the company behind Netflix's critically acclaimed dramas The Crown and Dept. Q. Amanda Coe, Steve Lightfoot, and Angela LaManna serve as writers and executive producers, while King Richard director Reinaldo Marcus Green will direct and executive produce. The modern-day setting could also let the series revisit Larsson's themes of corruption, power, and secrecy through a contemporary lens.

The adaptation previously faced a legal dispute over the rights to The Girl with the Dragon Tattoo between Sony and Banijay shortly after Sky announced the series in January 2026. However, the dispute has since been resolved, allowing the project to move forward with its growing cast.

The Girl with the Dragon Tattoo will premiere on Sky in several European territories, with Sony Pictures Television handling international distribution. An official release date has not been announced yet.

The Girl with the Dragon Tattoo is a dramatic mystery-thriller series based on the popular novel series Millenium by Stieg Larsson. This television adaptation explores the journey of a journalist named Mikael Blomkvist on the hunt for a missing woman with the help of a hacker named Lisbeth Salander.

Saints activate Kelvin Banks, Jordyn Tyson

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

The Saints offense will be getting a couple of reinforcements for Sunday’s game against the Browns.

They announced that they have activated left tackle Kelvin Banks and wide receiver Jordyn Tyson from injured reserve. Banks has been out since suffering an ankle injury in Week 2 and Tyson has yet to play this season due to a hamstring injury. Tyson was a first-round pick this season, so Sunday’s game will be his NFL debut.

As previously reported, the Saints released running back Kendre Miller in a corresponding move. They also released tight end Treyton Welch.

Kicker Charlie Smyth and running back Zamir White have been elevated from the practice squad on a temporary basis.

Netflix's Spy Thriller Black Doves Season 3 Hopes Officially Addressed By Creator

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Cher Thompson is a Senior Staff Writer at Screen Rant, where she covers everything from explosive reality TV moments to cast interviews about the twists and turns of scripted dramas. With a background in creative writing, marketing, and teaching, Cher knows how to spot both the storytelling genius and the guilty pleasures in pop culture. Her work blends wit with insight, making readers feel like they’re chatting with a friend who just happens to know way too much about TV.

After making waves with its exciting season 2, Black Doves appears to be gearing up for more action.

The Netflix spy thriller, starring Keira Knightley and Ben Whishaw, has developed a distinctive tone across its two short seasons, leaving viewers eager for more. However, whether Black Doves will return for season 3 remains undecided, but show creator Joe Barton may have sparked some hope.

In an interview with Variety, Barton explained that he's "in a writers' room for [Black Doves season 3] at the moment, and we're hoping." While this doesn't confirm that Netflix has ordered another season, it definitely strengthens the possibility.

While his answer doesn't necessarily mean that Netflix will move forward with another season, Black Doves season 2's ending left viewers yearning for more of the hit spy series. Knowing that Barton and his team are in a writers' room breaking down what would be next for Knightley's Helen Webb, especially after the shocking character death of the previous season, makes it clear that the creatives are interested in continuing the story. Now, the ball is in Netflix's court on whether the series moves forward.

Barton also discussed major character developments throughout Black Doves season 2, specificallythe decision to kill Agnes O'Caswey's Dani. While Knightley originally expressed public interest in Helen killing Dani after season 1, the decision to have Gabrielle Creevy's Eleanor do the job threw a wrench into the series' larger story. The creator shared that O'Casey's scheduling issues pushed the creative team to decide faster than expected on who would be behind Dani's demise and how it would happen.

Both Dani's death and Cecile's (Neve Campbell) were meant to shock the system, and Black Doves season 2 succeeded on both fronts. With viewers still reeling from the fallout of season 2, it's clear there are still stories to explore before the show can truly come to a natural close. With Helen's marriage to Wallace (Andrew Buchan) facing a major turning point, Sam's departure from her also leaves them on uncertain terms.

With far too much left to explore, season 3 feels less like a question and more like a necessity. Thankfully, the reception of Black Doves season 2, along with Barton's answers about his desire to keep going, feels like a great indicator of things to come.

'Unacceptable': Stephen Robinson's Aberdeen future addressed as Hibs smash Dons to move away from bottom spot

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Aberdeen boss Stephen Robinson insists he will face the challenge head-on as his side go into the international break in real trouble at the wrong end of the William Hill Premiership table.

Hibs started the evening three points behind Robinson’s side, but a dominant second-half performance in a 4-0 victory saw the Edinburgh club overtake their hosts in 10th place on goal difference.

Did you know with a Digital Subscription to The Scotsman, you can get unlimited access to the website including our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

The Dons showed promise early in the game and Hibs keeper Raphael Sallinger saved well from Jack Milne, who also hit the bar for Aberdeen before Jamie McGrath’s 25-yard strike found the target.

Aberdeen went all-out in search of an equaliser, but quickly fell 2-0 behind to Callum Wright, sparking calls for Robinson’s head from the home support. Further strikes from Thibault Klidje and Nicky Cadden completed the rout.

Robinson said: “It’s really, really disappointing, there’s no hiding away from it. It was an unacceptable performance and an unacceptable result.

“We haven’t taken our chances – Hibs had five or six chances and took four of them – that’s always the difference in football. We got the result we deserved.

“It’s a difficult situation – difficult for myself, the players, and the club. I will keep fighting until told otherwise.

“We have to be better than that – that’s not acceptable. We have to get players who want to fight and try to get us out of this situation.”

Hibs boss Marink Reedijk was full of praise for his side, saying: “It’s important to confirm that what we did last week was not a one-off.

“I think again we had some really good chances first half. We need to convert those but overall it’s a deserved win.

“I’m very happy not to concede, but it’s also important to me that individuals who have not played have come in and performed. I think about Alex Williams, who came in after 10 minutes and was fantastic.

“It shows the team is together and it was a team performance that showed where we want to go together.”

'We have to continue like this' - Hull City boss Sergej Jakirovic encouraged despite loss to Arsenal

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Hull City manager Sergej Jakirovic insisted his side are “still growing” following their 1-0 defeat to Arsenal.

The Tigers have not won a Premier League game since late August, but did put up a fight against the heavily-fancied Gunners.

Did you know with a Digital subscription to Yorkshire Post, you can get access to all of our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

Martin Odegaard struck with 18 minutes of the tight encounte remaining, tucking in the rebound after Tigers keeper Konstantinos Tzolakis could only parry Noni Madueke’s drive.

Jakirovic conceded Hull have lessons to learn - but is still “very pleased”.

He told Match of the Day: “We need to take lessons. I think this team is still growing. It is a very, very big experience for us to compete at this level and especially against Arsenal.

“We have to continue like this and to fight. We tried to press them and push them forward. They were trying to play short passes. It isn't easy because you spend a lot of energy and every player for them can pass you.

“I am very pleased. We have to continue, every game for us is a big lesson. It was bad fortune [for the goal]. Maybe we could stop the second ball for their goal.

“Sometimes I feel sorry because we had three home games and we had the lead and maybe could be more clever in these situations but it is all experience. We have to do everything in the next games to reach at least one win.”

Arsenal boss Mikel Arteta, meanwhile, said: “I’m delighted with the win because to go to the international break after winning is one of the best things for a coach or for a player.

“I think regarding the performance we were the better team. I think we had enough chances and situations to make the scoreline much bigger.

“We weren’t efficient enough to do that and then we have to rely on generating volume, generating a lot of situations in and around the box until we are able to break them down.

“This is exactly how we won the game and Martin, the way he scores is not the most beautiful one but the most effective one, which is a simple shot and then following the next action, and that was enough today to win again.”

American Healthcare REIT: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Relationship-driven sourcing lets American Healthcare REIT buy newer infill senior housing without bidding against rivals in public auctions.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 6, 2026, American Healthcare REIT (AHR +3.34%) announced an increase to its full-year normalized funds from operations (NFFO) guidance, a cash-flow performance metric for real estate investment trusts. For long-term investors, operational efficiency across senior housing properties is driving compounding cash flows. Three things management said stand out.

In the Senior Housing Operating Portfolio (SHOP), where American Healthcare REIT retains direct operational exposure, revenue per occupied room grew much faster than operating expenses per room. That widening spread expanded operating margins and drove substantially higher same-store net operating income (NOI)--property cash flow after operating costs--across the segment.

Rather than competing in public auctions where asset prices are bid up, management relies on direct relationships with operators to acquire properties. This off-market strategy allows the real estate investment trust (REIT) to buy newer senior housing communities in supply-constrained infill markets without overpaying.

Management reduced net debt relative to earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation, and amortization (EBITDA), expanding its funding capacity. By match-funding acquisitions through unsettled forward equity sales, the company supports its pipeline while preserving financial flexibility.

Aging demographics provide structural growth tailwinds for senior housing communities. Management relies on proprietary technology tools and regional operator partnerships to turn sector fundamentals into operational execution.

Capital deployment remains focused on disciplined match-funding for acquisitions in high-barrier markets while maintaining low leverage.

A memo circulated earlier this year claimed that technology executive Alice Huang was recruited by China’s Ministry of State Security to spy on Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang, according to multiple media reports.

The New York Times, which had previously said Nvidia’s chief executive had been the target of Chinese intelligence, was the first to report on the memo, which was reportedly based on U.S. intelligence. Forget Mayo: Give Tuna Salad A Major Flavor Boost With This Bottled Ingredient

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

We may receive a commission on purchases made from links.

If you're a fan of canned tuna and you get the right stuff, it can be perfectly tasty on a bed of lettuce — it's even one of the best foods to eat straight out of the can. It's a super high-protein food, and when you need a bit more flavor, a tuna salad is one of those simple meals that you can make quickly with just a little good mayo (our favorite being Kewpie) and mustard, maybe some pickle relish or diced cucumbers. That said, there are plenty of easy ways to take your plain tuna salad to the next level, but this is one you may have never thought of. You can skip the mayo completely and use a bottle of store-bought Caesar salad dressing in your next tuna salad. Our top pick for best Caesar salad dressing? Briannas Home Style Asiago Caesar Dressing, available at retailers like Walmart.

Using Caesar dressing in your tuna salad will highlight the salty, umami notes and give some nice vinegary undertones. Even bottled dressings typically have aged Italian cheeses, so they'll add amazing flavor that you can't get from most other off-the-shelf dressings. Plus, even though mayonnaise is often considered a tuna salad's best friend, when the salad is overly mixed (or even whipped with mayo), it can just turn out pasty and gooey and lose the texture of a perfectly good canned tuna altogether. With just a bit of Caesar dressing, mixed simply with a fork, you'll have a beautifully flavored tuna that's ready to add atop a bowl of greens or turn into a sandwich or a proper tuna melt.

Certainly not all Caesar dressings are created equal. Some may be a bit creamier or heavier on the garlic; some may contain anchovies, and some not. If you want more umami, go with something that has anchovies. And if you want the creamy factor that's closer to a traditional homemade tuna salad with mayo, you can often simply check for egg yolks or egg yolk powder on the ingredient list to get that extra hit of smooth, eggy flavor.

For the simplest Caesar tuna salad, per can of regular-sized tuna — which generally runs about five ounces (including the liquid) — you'll want about two and a half tablespoons of Caesar dressing. Whether you go with tuna in oil or in water is purely a personal choice. In oil, of course, the tuna will have more fat, so you may want to use less dressing. To add some crunch, diced onions, cukes, and celery are always a good idea, especially on a sandwich with a little cheese, lettuce, and tomato. Of course, you can add fresh or dried herbs and spices, like smoked paprika or even cayenne powder and hot sauce to add a little more intensity.

If you're making it to add to a full Caesar salad, you can simply toss canned tuna with some romaine lettuce, croutons, and Caesar dressing, then top with a little grated parmesan cheese. Caesar dressing also works great on baked salmon to keep it nice and moist. But when you're making your next simple tuna salad, you can maybe just go with a can of tuna and a few tablespoons of Caesar from the bottle, and you're good to go.

Zohran Mamdani and Puerto Rican Band Chuwi Discuss Gentrification, Music, and Making Change

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

The Puerto Rican band Chuwi, made up of siblings Lorén, Willy, and Wester and their best friend Adrián, has spent the last couple years achieving international fame through a major collab with Bad Bunny, also ending up as his opening act on tour. But they’ve also used their platform to speak out about Puerto Rico — their debut album, Libertad, from last month, tackles government corruption, the economic and social effects of colonialist policies, and more — and on Friday, they joined Mayor Zohran Mamdani in San Juan for a conversation about issues central to the islands’ future.

As part of a visit engaging local organizations and community leaders, Mamdani sat down with the musicians to hear more about their perspective. “I look forward to listening to Puerto Ricans to hear about what the struggles are that they’re facing and how New York City can be a partner in fighting for working people wherever they may call home,” the mayor told Rolling Stone.

The meeting was part of the annual Somos Conference, where New York State lawmakers meet with Latino advocates over four days in Puerto Rico. The conference’s goal is to create a space where attendees can speak directly with those in positions of power about what’s most important to their communities.

This year marks the 40th anniversary of the event, and it is customary for the mayor of New York City to attend. Last year, Mamdani flew down just days after he was elected. This year, he returned, 10 months into his first term, and seemed clear-eyed about what he wanted to talk about during his 72 hours in town. His central focus is bridging the gap between the archipelago and the nearly 600,000 Puerto Ricans who call New York City home, with Mayor Mamdani targeting issues that matter to both.

“What I’ve heard from Puerto Rican New Yorkers is a concern about displacement and gentrification, not only in the neighborhoods that they’ve helped to build in New York City, but also right here in Puerto Rico,” he says.The mayor met with various coalitions and activist groups, such as Defend Cabo Rojo, FURIA, La Tejedora, and PR No Se Vende (“Puerto Rico Is Not for Sale”) that have dedicated themselves to combatting those challenges. But before those meetings, he scheduled an equally important sit-down with Chuwi, given their mission to spread the word about Puerto Rico’s struggles through their music. Editor’s picks The 250 Greatest Albums of the 21st Century So Far The 100 Best TV Episodes of All Time The 500 Greatest Albums of All Time 100 Best Movies of the 21st Century

Chuwi became a household name after they collaborated on the song “WELTiTA” off Bad Bunny’s Grammy-winning DeBÍ TiRAR MáS FOToS. They went on to be the opening act of his 59-date world tour. On Libertad, the band went back to their protest music roots and leaned more than ever into the politics. They’ve addressed some of these themes on the album since their earliest EPs, but never as directly as the LP.

Lorén initiated the conversation with Mamdani by describing how she was in New York City the week before the 2025 election. She told him she was impressed by the enthusiasm she saw for his campaign, saying that she found it very inspiring as a Puerto Rican. “What you guys are doing is inspiring,” Mamdani said, complimenting how they use their music to spread important messages without compromising the artistic side.

“When you’re not at a desk, it’s easier to tell the truth,” he said. He noted that as artists, they have the unique privilege of being able to speak out and that their music is a connection to home for many fans. Indeed, even Bad Bunny himself said last year that listening to Chuwi during his time living in Los Angeles pre-DtMF made him homesick.

Willy took the opportunity to ask Mayor Mamdani about how he’s been able to achieve so much in his term so far: “Why is it you can do these policies that other mayors couldn’t?” Related Content Puerto Rico’s Next Big Music Movement Is Pop Introducing Rolling Stone’s National Affairs Newsletter  Chuwi’s Debut Album ‘Libertad’ Is As Powerful As It Is Catchy Mayor Mamdani and Nas Have a Heart to Heart About Keeping NYC the Home of Hip Hop

“When you get elected you get disconnected,” Mamdani said. He explained that leaders can become cut off from the people they serve, and that the environment can turn into tinted SUVs and private hotel rooms. He gave examples of things he does “intentionally” to stay connected, like biking around the city. “Institutions change slower than people,” and too much patience sometimes inadvertently leads to complacency. “The more you stay with the people the more you stay impatient. You have to keep [them] around you,” he says.

Chuwi pointed out how skeptical many citizens are about politicians, and how cynicism has become the prevalent feeling, especially in a Puerto Rico that has been suffering economically for almost two decades, even before Hurricane María dealt a blow to its infrastructure in 2017.

Mamdani acknowledged that feeling, and said Puerto Ricans might be skeptical because “they know about history.” He said he’s aware of the gap between what politicians promise and what citizens actually expect them to deliver on. “How do you collapse that separation?” he said, sharing that he feels “inspiration” is a stronger force than “disillusionment” and that he tries to wake up every day “ready to fight” and to “use pressure in a productive way.”

Aware of how heated things can get in politics, and having been on the receiving end of a fair share of criticism, Willy asked how the mayor deals with mud-slinging and negative rhetoric in politics. Mamdani said that treating people who disagree with him harshly or with judgment leads to both sides feeling like there has to be a winner or loser in the debate. He stressed the importance of speaking to people who aren’t just “those who feel comfortable with you.”

Mamdani then pivoted to asking Chuwi about their future plans. The band said that they’ll be returning to cities they visited over the summer, hinting at a big tour of their own. Mamdani playfully reminded them he used to be an artist as well — as an indie rapper going by the alias Mr. Cardamom — and reminisced about how rough it was the first few years. “I was the opener to the opener to the opener,” he joked. “I once performed at 5 p.m. for a band that was playing at 9 p.m. I was singing to the seat savers.”He said he was delighted by the possibility of Chuwi going to different countries with their music, because as he put it, “through that, others will hear and see their own struggles.”

Lorén thanked the mayor for meeting with them, sharing that their grandparents were one of the families that moved to New York City during the post-WWII migration wave, and in fact got married in the city. Mamdani stressed the importance of Puerto Ricans in the history of New York, especially in organized labor movements. Lorén shared that her grandfather worked at a textile factory in the city.

Willy capped off the meeting by thanking Mayor Mamdani for meeting with the organizations working on the ground in P.R. He stressed how important their goals were when it came to pushing for the abolition of Puerto Rico’s tax haven status, protecting the coastal maritime-terrestrial zone, and trying to stop large developments that threaten Puerto Rico’s habitats like Esencia in Cabo Rojo and Moncayo in Fajardo, among other causes. “Politicians [in Puerto Rico] won’t meet with them unless it’s in a court room or public hearings,” he said. Trending Stories A Jesuit Priest Explains How He Facilitated Bob Dylan’s Meeting With the Pope Richard Lloyd, Television's Virtuoso Guitarist, Dead at 75 Houston Mayor Limits Cooperation With ICE: ‘Elections Matter’ Good Riddance to the Cybertruck, the Chariot of the Dumbest Gods

Afterwards, Chuwi told Rolling Stone they felt inspired by the mayor’s words. “Being that he’s the mayor of New York, and New York has significant importance in Puerto Rico, it was super important to be able to discuss these matters with him,” says Wester.They also pushed back on critics who might accuse Mayor Mamdani of wading into issues outside his jurisdiction. “My counterargument is that we’re a colony of the United States, and New York is — and has always been — extremely important in Puerto Rico. We’re linked,” says Wester. “The rest of the world is watching what’s happening here on the island, yet our own politicians aren’t addressing it. I think it’s fair game.”

“It’s important to understand that this isn’t like the Financial Oversight Board. Just because people from outside — rather than locals — are addressing these issues, it doesn’t mean they’re coming here to exert control or anything like that,” Adrián says. “People hear ‘the United States is coming here to do things in Puerto Rico’ and immediately think, ‘Oh, Junta de Control Fiscal.’ No, it’s not [necessarily] that. It’s about creating a dialogue between the two sides.”As the meeting wrapped up and Chuwi said their goodbyes, the mayor snuck in one last question: are they doing a show in New York in the near future? Chuwi let Mayor Mamdani know they’ll be seeing each other again soon.

Consumer Staples Showdown: Weighing the Differences Between VDC and RSPS

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Vanguard delivered 21.3% returns over one year with lower costs, while Invesco's equal-weighting approach offers higher dividend income at 2.8%.

The Vanguard Consumer Staples ETF (VDC -1.16%) offers broad sector exposure with very low costs, while the Invesco S&P 500 Equal Weight Consumer Staples ETF (RSPS -1.34%) equally weights its holdings to reduce concentration risk.

Both exchange-traded funds provide a defensive anchor for portfolios by targeting non-discretionary goods, yet they differ sharply in construction. One relies on market-capitalization weighting, which can lead to top-heavy positions, whereas the other ensures that every company in the index carries an equal influence regardless of its market value.

Beta measures price volatility relative to the S&P 500; beta is calculated from monthly returns over the available fund history (up to five years). The 1-yr return represents total return over the trailing 12 months. Dividend yield is the trailing-12-month distribution yield.

The Vanguard fund is the more affordable choice for long-term investors with its 0.09% expense ratio. While the Invesco fund is more expensive at 0.4%, it offers a noticeably higher payout for income-focused investors.

The Vanguard Consumer Staples ETF holds 103 positions, providing broad exposure, with 98% of the portfolio concentrated in the consumer defensive sector. Its largest holdings include Walmart Inc (WMT -1.72%) at 13.86%, Costco Wholesale Corp (COST -1.13%) at 12.12%, and Procter & Gamble Co. (PG -1.02%) at 9.90%. It was launched in 2004 and manages its portfolio without specific quirks. Vanguard Consumer Staples ETF has paid $4.88 per share over the trailing 12 months, which, on its recent ~$246.9 share price, works out to a 2% yield.

The Invesco S&P 500 Equal Weight Consumer Staples ETF is more focused, with 34 holdings, and employs an equal-weighting strategy to prevent any single stock from dominating the fund. Its portfolio is 94% consumer defensive, with 3% in both consumer cyclical and basic materials. Its largest positions include Kimberly-Clark Corp (KMB -1.84%) at 5.47%, Kroger Co. (KR -2.49%) at 3.33%, and Walmart at 3.32%. It was launched in 2006. Invesco S&P 500 Equal Weight Consumer Staples ETF has paid $0.89 per share over the trailing 12 months, which, on its recent ~$32.0 share price, works out to a 2.8% yield.

For more guidance on ETF investing, check out the full guide at this link.

When comparing the Vanguard Consumer Staples ETF (VDC) and the Invesco S&P 500 Equal Weight Consumer Staples ETF (RSPS), investors should consider several key factors. Let's see what they tell us about each fund.

First, we should examine each fund's strategy. VDC is a market-weighted fund that includes consumer staples stocks from the large-cap, mid-cap, and small-cap segments. RSPS, on the other hand, is an equal-weighted fund that includes consumer staples stocks from the S&P 500. VDC holds about 100 stocks, while RSPS holds far fewer, around 40 in all.

Two other important factors are historical performance and income potential. Over the last five years, VDC has generated a total return of 45%, equating to a compound annual growth rate (CAGR) of 7.7%. RSPS, by contrast, has delivered a total return of 14%, with a 2.7% CAGR. Both funds have underperformed the S&P 500 over this period. As for income, RSPS delivers the higher yield, at 2.8% versus only 2% for VDC.

One final factor to weigh is cost. Here, VDC gains an advantage. Its 0.09% annual expense ratio is far lower than RSPS's 0.40%. For context, if a person were to invest $10,000 in both funds, they should expect to pay $9 in annual fees for their VDC shares but $40 for their RSPS shares.In summary, VDC and RSPS both hold consumer staples stocks, but there are important differences in their structures. Many investors will favor VDC given its lower fees and superior historical returns. However, those seeking an equal-weighted approach or higher yields may still consider RSPS.

Jake Lerch has positions in Procter & Gamble. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Costco Wholesale and Walmart. The Motley Fool recommends Kroger. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Forget Lilly and Novo: Pfizer Is Planning a Wave of Phase 3 Obesity Trials

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Everyone is talking about the GLP-1 battle between Eli Lilly and Novo Nordisk, but Pfizer wants in on the fight, too.

Eli Lilly (LLY -0.79%) is much loved on Wall Street because of its highly successful GLP-1 weight-loss business. Novo Nordisk (NVO +0.03%) is much less appreciated because it lost the GLP-1 lead to Eli Lilly. But they aren't the only companies competing in this still-developing drug category. Pfizer (PFE -0.20%), which is deeply out of favor among investors, could be a surprise contender as it advances its GLP-1 drugs through the approval process. Here's what you need to know.

Eli Lilly's stock is up more than 420% over the past five years, as of this writing. While it was second to market with a GLP-1 weight-loss drug, it quickly overtook Novo Nordisk's lead. Being passed by Eli Lilly hasn't gone over well with investors, which is why Novo Nordisk's stock is down 27% over the past five years. However, Novo Nordisk's GLP-1 pill has been performing well, so there's still hope for the business.

Left out in the cold has been Pfizer. Despite being a highly respected pharmaceutical company, Pfizer's stock is down nearly 30% over the past five years. A big part of the problem is that Pfizer's internally developed GLP-1 drug was dropped, which was a huge black eye for the company. Investors may be taking too dim a view of the future here.

Competition in the healthcare sector is intense, and innovation doesn't always work out as hoped. Well-run companies know how to deal with both issues. This is why it is important to note that Pfizer quickly turned around and acquired a company with an attractive GLP-1 drug still in development.

Buying a drug that's still in development isn't a guarantee of success. So the company still has a lot of work to do, but it is work that Pfizer knows well. The company now has multiple clinical trials going on in the weight-loss space. The key to Pfizer's approach is convenience.

Currently, Novo Nordisk and Eli Lilly GLP-1 weight-loss drugs are taken either weekly by injection or daily by pill. Pfizer is looking to offer patients a monthly shot option. In recent trials, Pfizer's GLP-1 drug helped to reduce weight by 12% even after shifting to monthly shots. Patients don't like taking shots, so selling a GLP-1 shot with a monthly cadence may be highly desirable. Once-a-month dosing may even beat daily pills, since it means remembering to take medicine far less often. The power of convenience can't be overstated.

All in, Pfizer has 10 late-stage trials in its weight-loss portfolio. Each of those trials will help bring it closer to having an approved GLP-1 product to sell. But the real investment story here isn't the trials; it's that Wall Street appears to have written Pfizer off as an also-ran. That's particularly notable for dividend investors, given the stock's lofty 5.5% yield. The average pharma stock yields 1.3%, while Eli Lilly's yield is even lower at 0.5%. Novo Nordisk's yield is roughly 4.4%, which is attractive but not as high as Pfizer's.

Maintaining and growing the dividend over time are key priorities for Pfizer. While it is working from behind right now, the company's long history of success in the drug space suggests it will eventually turn things around. And the late-stage GLP-1 trials it has underway strongly suggest that the inflection point could be closer than Wall Street currently believes. If you are a long-term dividend investor, now could be a good time to get to know Pfizer. The yield may not be as attractive if you wait until the company actually enters the GLP-1 race with an approved product.

Reuben Gregg Brewer has no position in any of the stocks mentioned. The Motley Fool has positions in and recommends Eli Lilly, Novo Nordisk, and Pfizer. The Motley Fool has a disclosure policy.

Your EV's battery is big enough to run your house — here's what stops most people

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Getting the energy into the house can be complicated and expensive.

Most EV batteries have significantly more capacity than a standard home battery, so it makes sense to consider running the house with your car. But there are caveats. You have to consider the EV's battery capacity, power output and vehicle-to-home (V2H) compatibility. Battery capacity is measured in kilowatt-hours and helps determine how long you can keep things running. Power output is measured in kilowatts and determines which appliances you can run at the same time. The V2H system also needs to handle the brief startup surge when appliances such as air-conditioners and refrigerators kick on. The numbers are important, especially for startup surge.

Those numbers don't matter much, however, if you can't get energy out of the vehicle. To turn your EV into a home backup system, it must support bidirectional operation and come with a compatible V2H system. A typical charging connection doesn't necessarily work in reverse. Your charger also must be bidirectional and include DC-to-AC inverter hardware and an islanding/transfer switch.

V2H, powering your whole home, is often confused with vehicle-to-load (V2L), which powers individual devices using an EV. With V2L, you can power a refrigerator or your favorite electric foot massager. Some EVs can supply household circuits through a built-in power outlet and compatible transfer equipment, but this requires an approved installation and doesn't mean you can plug your EV into a standard wall outlet. All of this is to say that it's a little more complicated than a standard plug-and-play setup. And it doesn't end there.

Even with the perfect car, a whole-home or selected-circuit backup requires compatible power conversion equipment and, of course, a safe way to disengage the home from utility power. The point is to power your home, not energize grid wiring during an outage. Setup can get a little pricey as well. Depending on the system, you're looking at a bidirectional charger, inverter and a gateway, plus the electrical work and potential permits. You may also have to fork over more cash for panel or wiring upgrades.

Everyday limitations complicate things further. The car has to be at home, obviously. It has to be connected and sufficiently charged. Every unit of energy used by the house is energy no longer available for driving. Air-conditioning and electric heating are heavier workloads and will gobble up runtime substantially. You have to consider battery wear too. It shouldn't overshadow emergencies when you have to do what you have to, but frequent usage is a compromise in the long run.

Compatibility is key, and adaptation is necessary when compatibility is lacking. You can't play a PS5 with an Xbox controller right out of the gate. You need a third-party adapter. The same holds true for EVs powering homes. Before purchasing an EV with home backup in mind, make sure the required equipment is available and that an installer can commission the system at your address. House hardware, local availability and vehicle support all have to be in sync.

Compatible GM EVs work with the GM Energy PowerShift Charger and V2H Enablement Kit. Kia offers an EV9 setup using Wallbox's Quasar 2 charger and Power Recovery Unit. Tesla's Cybertruck supports PowerShare through a compatible home installation, and Tesla now lists Model 3 and Model Y support with Powerwall 3. Unfortunately, most of these are not interchangeable. Everything has to match. And all of them cost thousands of dollars.

Where you live plays a role as well. Some manufacturers' bidirectional services are market-specific. For instance, Polestar introduced bidirectional charging in PoleStar 3 EVs for those living in California. So don't get excited about EV-to-home features only to make the purchase and learn you're in the wrong state. Make sure the model and model year support outage backup at your address, and make sure you grab an installation quote.

Little Rascals Star Bug Hall Shares Financial Plea Ahead of Baby No. 6

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Former child star Bug Hall—best known for his work in The Little Rascals—created a fundraiser asking for financial aid as he and wife Jill prepare to welcome baby no. 6: "There was no other choice."

Bug Hall is asking for help during a desperate time.

Nine months after The Little Rascals actor announced that he'd taken a “vow of poverty” while living off-the-grid with his family, he launched a GoFundMe asking for financial assistance ahead of the birth of his and wife Jill’s sixth baby.

“It's now six years since I self-cancelled, and I have avoided doing one of these in all that time,” Hall shared on the fundraiser’s page Nov. 5. “I always said this was my choice, and so I didn't want to offload the glorious burden of it unless there was no other choice. Well here we are.”

The former child star added that his soon-to-be family of eight—who live in a camper van in Mountain View, Ark.—“made it onto the new land in record time,” but are a “few materials short of having things working right.”

As Hall (real name Brandon Hall) admitted, “I've fallen a little behind on the few bills we have (phone, propane for the camper, and an unexpected electric hookup service fee).”

While the 41-year-old—best known for playing Alfalfa in the 1994 film The Little Rascals—emphasized that his family is “all overjoyed to be where they are” and they’re “even enjoying roughing it,” time is currently not on their side.

“Baby [No. 6] is less than a month away,” he wrote, “and I really need some help getting the last few things buttoned up beforehand.”

“My hope is to finalize everything here over the next two weeks,” the Get a Clue star added, “so I can take some work until the baby arrives.

But Hall—who previously shared in an April 2025 YouTube video that he and his wife were raising their five kids in a “radical Catholic extremist life”—noted that he welcomes and any all forms of support, not only financially.

“Times are hard for everyone, and I'm no more deserving than anyone else,” he wrote, “but I ask in your charity that you help where you can, whether monetarily, by sharing this, or by prayer and sacrifice for the Hall-Barnetts of the Holy Cross.”

Hall’s plea for assistance comes nearly one year after he detailed to the Daily Mail why he'd taken a “vow of poverty," revealing that he'd given up the money he earned from his acting career, which he quit in 2020 following his arrest for huffing air duster cans. (He told the Daily Mail he faced no charges.)

“My goal is to maintain a life as free of any need for an income as possible,” he told the outlet in an interview published Jan. 28. “If there's a financial need that comes up, I'll go take some work or do an odd job, for cash to fulfill that need.”

As Hall and his family continue to build their life off-the-grid, keep reading for more stars who have chosen to leave Hollywood…

Now, the Tony nominee—who still occasionally takes on TV and film projects—owns a theater venue and gets involved with the local community.

DraftKings: 3 Key Takeaways for Long-Term Investors

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

The self-funded push into predictions rests on how quickly new customers pay back their acquisition cost, which the article examines closely.

On its third-quarter 2026 earnings call, held on Nov. 6, 2026, DraftKings (DKNG -21.30%) announced an increased 2026 adjusted EBITDA investment in its predictions business of $650 million to $700 million to capture rapid customer growth. This aggressive push into prediction markets alters short-term profitability while creating a low-volatility revenue stream. Understanding how capital gets allocated between core sports betting and new product lines is vital to evaluating the company's long-term earnings trajectory. Three key takeaways from management's comments highlight the strategic direction.

Management expanded capital deployment into prediction markets because customer acquisition metrics indicated faster cost recovery than originally projected. Quick payback cycles mitigate potential legal risks around event contracts while expanding the user base across non-sports categories.

DraftKings relies on its core sportsbook and gaming operations to generate cash, allowing it to fund new ventures internally without taking on debt or issuing shares. Higher net revenue margins and disciplined overhead costs support this self-funded expansion strategy.

Investments in proprietary game content and application updates accelerated user acquisition in the digital casino segment. Management expects these initial promotional expenditures to convert into expanded gross profit margins as player cohorts mature.

DraftKings is expanding its revenue base through cross-selling across its digital sports and predictions platform, setting up top-line expansion for fiscal 2027.

Integrated technology ownership and direct marketing discipline give the business long-term pricing power while management monitors state regulatory developments and capital allocation rules.

Will Josh O’Connor Be The Next James Bond?

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Let’s clear up any potential misunderstandings: there are no official announcements on who will play James Bond at this time. But a British tabloid says that a conversation at a pub has revealed the top-secret casting—if an anonymously sourced rumor is to be believed.

A long list of stars are reportedly auditioning for the 007 role, but even former James Bond Pierce Brosnan says the casting process is a mystery. “I’m just the man in the street who is eager to see who is going to play this epic role of cinematic history,” Brosnan tells Vanity Fair. “All the men who are on the list now are actors I admire.”

According to UK publication The Sun, the decision is rumored to have been made: a recent colleague of Josh O’Connor, a 36-year-old actor from Cheltenham, Gloucestershire, says Disclosure Day star has been cast in the role. If true, Turner beat out likely contenders including Callum Turner and Aaron Taylor-Johnson.

Josh O'Connor at UK premiere for Disclosure Day on June 04, 2026.

The news comes as a bit of a game of telephone. Until last week, O'Connor was in the case of Golden Boy, Clifford Odets' 1937 drama about a violin player turned boxer in Depression-era New York City. Citing the claims of one of O'Connor's cast mates, an unnamed source tells The Sun “I bumped into one of Josh’s Golden Boy cast mates at The French House."

“Out of nowhere, and oiled by a few glasses of lager, they blurted out that Josh was the next James Bond."

When the source pushed back, the thespian grew more insistent. “They said that he had confided in them about getting the role when the cast of Golden Boy had celebratory drinks after the play finished its run on Halloween," a claim that implies the decision has been made for over a week.

Josh O'Connor, winner of the Outstanding Lead Actor In A Drama Series award for The Crown, at the 73rd Primetime Emmy Awards on September 19, 2021.

If this rumor comes to fruition, it would not be the first time O'Connor took on an iconic UK role. In 2021, he took home the outstanding lead actor Emmy for his role as then-Prince, now-King Charles III, in Netflix series The Crown. While in his role as the future king, he was encouraged to tap into a side that one might not expect.

“It’s Prince Charles as Walter White," he told The Guardian in 2020, making a reference to crime series Breaking Bad. "Our job was to make people feel sorry for [Charles], so that in season four, when he turns into this at times monstrous character, we understand how he got there.” A heel turn that isn't unBond, especially the darker Bond we grew used to with Daniel Craig. (Craig, in fact, has a direct connection to O’Connor, as the pair co-starred in Knives Out: Wake Up Dead Man.)

Speaking with the BBC, O'Connor did not confirm the casting rumor—but he didn't deny it either. (Vanity Fair has reached out to O'Connor's representatives for comment, but has not yet received a response.)

"To be considered in that same sort of bracket is extraordinary," O'Connor said this week.

"It's an incredibly bizarre pinch me moment...even being uttered in that same sentence and being considered for something like that. But I am afraid, there is nothing to say beyond that."

Guiseley manager Ian Richards on FA Cup shock – we deserved it

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

The Lions reached the second round of the FA Cup in dramatic fashion, but there was nothing fortunate about their 1-0 win over Sutton United.

The only goal of the game was a Jordan Thewlis penalty in the fourth added minute, but a home win was a fair reflection of how Guiseley outperformed a team two divisions above them in the Conference.

Did you know with a Digital subscription to Yorkshire Post, you can get access to all of our premium content, as well as benefiting from fewer ads, loyalty rewards and much more.

"I think the performance deserved the result," commented manager Richards.

"I know we scored in the closing minutes with a penalty but their first shot on target came from 25 yards in the 92nd minute. I thought we had some excellent chances. We had a couple of penalty shouts prior to that one.

"We had excellent chances in the first and second half where we could have won the game.

"What I was really pleased with against a top National League side who played the strongest team, changed the personnel (during the game), changed the shape of the side that kept a clean sheet.

"Again we called on sub to continue that momentum, so another squad effort and the club's really proud of them.

"They've created history, they've created memories and they've created even more connections with the supporters and people within the club.

"They're a really honest group and I'm so pleased for them.

"For me the cups are for the fans, the club and the players to have those memories.

"Obviously as a manager my priority will always be the league and I've got to think about that very soon. But for the players and the club we need to enjoy these memories.

"It's a short career as a player but it's the third time this club has got to the second round of the FA Cup so we need to mark and celebrate the history that's been made.

"I thought the last 10 minutes we've been so much stronger.

"They really struggled with the pace and the energy that we're playing with. Our identity came through and that's what wins the penalty.

"It was a bit like when we were schoolboys. He's won it, he takes it. Cam Wilson's our penalty-taker but Jordan's good from the penalty spot.

"I was very calm. Even when the (unused) players ran on and celebrated. I was very calm there."

And on top of the win which puts Northern Premier League Guiseley in Monday's second round draw – the lowest-ranked side whose place was confirmed on Saturday - there was another element which delighted Richards.

"The one thing I've been disappointed in is the brilliant run we've had is the last five games we've conceded goals, maybe soft goals or preventable goals," he said.

"Clean sheets win you promotions, win you big cup games like this. We kept a clean sheet. We always know that from front players and attacking players as well, we're going to score at some point."

Patriots activate Mack Hollins from IR

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

The Patriots won’t have A.J. Brown or Romeo Doubs on Sunday, but they will be getting another wide receiver back from injury against the Packers.

They have activated Mack Hollins from injured reserve. Hollins returned to practice this week after missing the last four games with a calf injury.

Hollins had 15 catches for 209 yards in four appearances before his injury. He had 46 catches for 550 yards and two touchdowns in his first season with the Patriots.

Brown will miss his second straight game with a knee injury and Doubs remains on injured reserve. Pop Douglas, Efton Chism, and Kyle Williams join Hollins as the available receivers on the 53-man roster for New England.

Why Does The Cat In The Hat Have A Flip Phone? Bill Hader Has Thoughts

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

Join The Club for quick access. Enter your email below and we'll send confirmation, and sign you up to our newsletter.

Carmen Electra Went Full Catwoman For Halloween (But I Didn't See Kat Dennings As A Carrot Coming)

I Wondered Why Nathan Fielder Was Picked For You Can See Everything. His Answer Confused Me

I Just Found Out What Christopher Nolan Made Universal’s Head Honcho Do When He Pitched The Odyssey

Unlock instant access to exclusive member features.

Get full access to premium articles, exclusive features and a growing list of member rewards.

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

One thing I love about book-to-screen adaptations of classics like The Cat In The Hat is how each new iteration reimagines a character we already love. Bill Hader’s version comes over two decades after the Mike Myers movie, and one peculiar new thing I noticed about his animated take on the character is the fact that he has a flip phone. Random, right?

When CinemaBlend spoke to Hader about his latest role, I had to ask about the little detail I noticed about his The Cat In The Hat. Here’s how the SNL alum responded:

Because Cat in the Hat's real. Cat in the Hat doesn't need to fit in with whatever's happening now, you know?

For some reason, it just feels right that The Cat would have a flip phone (and one that even has a crystal-clear video chat function). Plus, this detail about The Cat definitely adds to his unique character in a cute way, as it's revealed in the film that he’s been doing house calls to bring play into the lives of kids for multiple generations. So, perhaps the flip phone tells us that he's a bit old school. Bill Hader also said this about the topic:

I think it was funny that it was a flip phone. I have friends who have flip phones, and they remember things better than me. They are not as distracted as me. They seem much happier than me.

Hey, maybe The Cat is onto something. I mean the character is all about bringing playtime and wonder to kids. Why would he need a smartphone for that?

These days, a lot more people are ditching their smartphones for more primitive technology like flip phones to get away from things like social media addiction. Chris Pine, for example, had one for four years before switching to the latest technology and finding himself wanting to “immediately go back”. Other famous people known to have a flip phone are Christopher Nolan, Anna Wintour and Rihanna – so The Cat can count himself among icons.

A lot of other Y2K trends have been coming back lately, too. For example, both Kylie Jenner and Ariana Grande recently admitted their love for wired earphones (and I’m totally with them). I’ve seen a lot of younger people out with their digital cameras at concerts too. When you think about it, The Cat In The Hat’s “dumb phone” is very in with the kids.

There’s already The Cat In The Hat trend; maybe this could be part of a new wave of less phone dependence and more focus on fun and creativity, like the character itself has been teaching kids since the picture book came out back in 1957.

You can check out the new The Cat In The Hat movie in theaters now.

Path of Exile 2 Shadow class preview: Rogues do it from behind!

📅 2026-11-07  |  Read Source →

What's better than killing your enemies before they even know what happened?

Destructoid is at ExileCon 2026 all the way in Auckland, New Zealand, checking out what awaits us in Path of Exile 2‘s fabled 1.0 release as well as what’s new for the first game.

And the first of those relates to the new Shadow class—a stealthy rogue sneaking up on entire groups of enemies and taking them out before their first strike lands. I played it for about 15 minutes yesterday, experiencing several of its exceptionally complex and difficult-to-master abilities, and using them to take on large mob waves in less-than-spectacular fashion. But that is exactly the thing with the Shadow: even if you do not know what you’re doing, the class is pure eye candy, and everything that you succeed in makes it seem like you’re some kind of pro.

The Shadow specializes in stealth and can turn invisible on a whim. This invisibility breaks when you attack or when you get too close to an enemy or if the enemies discover your shadow clone and destroy it.

While invisible, you can cast traps, setting and piling them up on a large group of enemies and ensuring your victory before the battle even happens. The traps come in many different forms: poison gases, concussion grenades, and so on, and each interacts with others in cool and unique ways. For example, casting a poison gas trap and combining it with an explosive trap results in a massive burst of flame that ignites enemies and deals a ton of fire damage. Concussion grenades can further disperse these flames and gas, significantly increasing AOE.

However, being the absolute bot that I am, I went for a one-button run, using only the daggers and the class’s shadow clone jutsu to deal incredible damage in a large AOE using nothing but my stabs since, well, the shadows aren’t just there for show. They repeat most attacks that you do yourself, meaning that you can create a dozen of them, all of which will lambast your target with the same dagger attack that you yourself used. And they do it in a way that’d put League of Legends‘ Zed to shame, and Naruto couldn’t even hold a candle to them.

The Shadow is thus all about setting up the attack and executing it with lightning speed. You create a bunch of clones and go all-in on your enemy together with them, lay a bunch of traps using stealth, or both.

Of course, since I had a very limited time with the class, I couldn’t really learn the ropes in time and maximize its capabilities, but I’m pretty sure this could turn out to be the most powerful class in the entire game. The strength, naturally, comes from its complexity, meaning the best players are going to find wild ways of utilizing the Shadow to decimate even the most challenging enemies and bosses in basically single strikes.

What I really loved about it overall is that it finally brings the Rogue fantasy into PoE2. We can be proper assassins and thieves (called infiltrators here) and do what rogues do best: kill our foes before they ever know what’s going on.

The Shadow drops together with Path of Exile 2‘s 1.0 update on Dec. 11.
